# Parallels index: the Ugaritic corpus

The key findings (written by the coordinator when the run ends), then the table: every **הקבלה** row of the annotated
files, filed by its first Bible verse (generated by `build_parallels_index.py`; do not edit the table by hand).

## Key findings

## Key findings of Round 5 run 3 (2026-10-31: Aqhat, Kirta, the Rephaim, KTU 1.161, 1.40, 1.119, 1.39 – 1,424 lines)

**For the Bible**
1. **Gen 1:2's `מְרַחֶפֶת` has its Ugaritic cognate in Aqhat, of birds of prey over a victim.** *rḫp* (D): Anat's eagles
   "hovering" over Aqhat as he eats (1.18 IV:20, 31 `נשרים מרחפים`; IV:21 `ארחף`; IV:32 `רִחֲפָה ענת`) and over Danel's
   house of mourning (1.19 I:32). With 1.108:8 (Anat hovering) the verb is always a winged goddess or her birds, never a
   wind over waters: Deut 32:11's eagle over its young is the Bible's closer use; Gen 1:2 [cognate], a divergence of
   subject and setting. (The reviewer corrected IV:32 from a participle to the past: the tablet does not give Gen 1:2's
   form.)
2. **The son's duties (1.17 I:26–33, four copies)** – the stele of the father's god, his smoke from the earth, taking his
   hand when he is drunk, carrying him when sated with wine, plastering his roof, washing his clothes: ↔ **Gen 9:20–27**
   [divergence] (in Ugarit a duty without shame; in Genesis the setting of Ham's sin); 2 Sam 18:18, Gen 35:14, 20 (the
   stele; Absalom `אֵין לִי בֵן`).
3. **The childless righteous judge**: Danel (`דָּנִאֵל`, Ezek 14:14, 20) at the gate judging the widow and the orphan
   (1.17 V:8; Ex 22:21, Deut 10:18, Jer 5:28); the god fed as a guest (Gen 18:1–8: `וַיִּשָּׂא עֵינָיו וַיַּרְא` = *b nši ʿnh
   w yphn*); the son granted after incubation (Gen 15:2–4, 28:11; 1 Sam 1).
4. **Immortality offered and refused** (1.17 VI:26–38): Anat offers `בַּל מָוֶת`, Aqhat answers *mt kl amt*, "the death of
   all men I shall die" ↔ Gen 2:17, 3:4 `מוֹת תָּמוּת`, Num 16:29 `כְּמוֹת כָּל הָאָדָם`, Gen 3:22.
5. **Blood, rain and the towns** (1.19): Danel's curse on the rain "seven years … eight" ↔ 2 Sam 1:21, 1 Kgs 17:1; the
   towns nearest the murder cursed "for all generations" ↔ Deut 21:1–9 (there atoned), Gen 4:10–12 [divergence]; the
   remains gathered from the eagles and buried (1 Sam 31:13).
6. **Kirta and the Torah's war laws** (1.14 II:43–50, IV:21–28): the new bridegroom *goes out* to war and leaves his wife
   to another – the same three words as Deut 20:7, 24:5 (*yṣʾ*, *ḥdṯ*, *ṣbʾ*) with the opposite rule; the besieger refuses
   the peace gifts (Deut 20:10–11 [divergence]); the dream that a king obeys word for word, a god's offer of wealth
   refused for an heir (1 Kgs 3:5–15; Gen 15:1–3); the conditional vow at a goddess's shrine (Gen 28:20–22; *ndr*
   `נָדַר` [cognate]) – forgotten, and punished by illness (Deut 23:22, Num 30).
7. **The youngest made firstborn** (1.15 III:16 *abkrn*, `אֲבַכְּרֶנָּה` ↔ Deut 21:16 `לְבַכֵּר` [cognate], against the
   Torah's ban; Gen 48); the heir who sucks the milk of Athirat and Anat (Isa 60:16).
8. **"Do gods die?"** (1.16 I–II): the children of the sick king ↔ **Ps 82:6–7** `אָכֵן כְּאָדָם תְּמוּתוּן`; the healer
   Šaʿtiqat pinched from clay by El (*qrṣ ṭiṭ* `קָרַץ טיט` ↔ Job 33:6 `מֵחֹמֶר קֹרַצְתִּי`; Gen 2:7's `יָצַר` / `עָפָר`
   absent: the verb is the finding); Yaṣṣib's charge "you do not judge the widow's case" (Isa 1:23, Ps 82:3, 2 Sam 15:4)
   and the father's curse on the son (Gen 9:25, 49:4).
9. **The Rephaim** (`רְפָאִים` [cognate]): in 1.20–1.22 the dead kings are guests who ride to Danel's threshing floors and
   feast seven days; in 1.161 they are summoned for the dead king's burial, the throne weeps, Šapšu sends him "down …
   to the dust" (Isa 14:9 [divergence]: there the Rephaim rise from thrones; Isa 47:1; Gen 3:19), and the rite ends in
   "peace" for the new king and the gates (Ps 122:7). Ps 106:28 `זִבְחֵי מֵתִים` = the tablet's title "the sacrifice of
   the shades" (*ẓlm* `צְלָלִים`, not `צֶלֶם`).
10. **KTU 1.40 and Lev 16 / Lev 4**: the offering is "borne" (*nšʾ*) – **up** to the father of the gods, not away into the
    wilderness, and it is a donkey or a ram, not a goat; the city's groups by status (men, women, the resident alien
    `גֵּר`, the king) as Lev 4's; sins listed as possibilities ("whether by your anger, your impatience …", Num 21:4
    `וַתִּקְצַר נֶפֶשׁ`); **the root כפר is not on the tablet** (*npy* `טָהֳרָה`) – Lev 16's word is the finding.
11. **KTU 1.119**: the prayer for a besieged city (Ps 121:1, Judg 11:30, Gen 28:22 the tithe); **the famous "firstborn"
    offering is not on the tablet** (*dkr* "male", not *bkr*): 2 Kgs 3:27 is a parallel of situation only.

**For the ancient mind:** a son defined by his duties to a father alive and dead; a goddess who covets a bow, offers a man
immortality and kills him through her eagles, and weeps; a land that dies with its heir; a father who cuts open birds
to find a bone to bury; a sister who arms herself under a woman's dress; a king who wants sons more than gold, obeys a
dream to the letter, forgets one vow and sickens, and whose children cannot believe a king can die; a god who makes a
healer from clay when all the gods are silent; the dead kings who come to feast and are sent down to the dust; a whole
city, its foreigners included, purified with a donkey borne up to the gods.


Round 4 run 1, 2026-10-23 (first draft: the whole Baal Cycle KTU 1.1–1.6 and eight short texts, 1,749 lines; the
fifth-day dossier in `3-תיק-היום-החמישי/`). Every item is in the table below with its line and tag.

**For the fifth day (Gen 1:20–23)**
1. **The sea monsters are enemies, never creatures.** *tnn* is the defeated or muzzled foe: Anat's boast "I muzzled
   the *tnn*" (1.3 III:40, asseverative *l*, not a question: Isa 51:9's question form is a [divergence]); Šapšu rules
   over "in the sea – Arš and the *tnn*" (1.6 VI:51 = Isa 27:1 `הַתַּנִּין אֲשֶׁר בַּיָּם`); 1.82:1 beside *mḫṣ* (Job
   26:12; uncertain syntax). Gen 1:21 `וַיִּבְרָא … אֶת הַתַּנִּינִם הַגְּדֹלִים` makes the same word a created kind: the
   verb `בָּרָא` against Ugarit's *mḫṣ* / *šbm* is the finding. **1.83:8 has *tan*, not *tnn*** (CUC = KTU³): the "tnn
   bound and muzzled" there is an emendation and is cited only as such.
2. **Leviathan's adjectives belong to a *bṯn*.** *ltn bṯn brḥ … bṯn ʿqltn* (1.5 I:1–2; 1.3 III:41–42) has the very
   adjectives of Isa 27:1 (`נָחָשׁ בָּרִחַ … נָחָשׁ עֲקַלָּתוֹן`); Isaiah kept the name and the adjectives and changed
   the noun to `נָחָשׁ` (*bṯn* = `פֶּתֶן` [cognate], Ps 58:5, 91:13). Ugarit's mythic serpent is never *nḥš*: *nḥš* is
   the snake of the charms (1.100, 1.107).
3. **Cosmic vocabulary that Gen 1–2 shares:** *thw* `תֹּהוּ` (1.5 I:15, Mot's appetite) [cognate]; *thmtm* `שְׁתֵּי
   הַתְּהוֹמוֹת` at El's dwelling (1.4 IV:22; Gen 49:25, Deut 33:13 `תְּהוֹם רֹבֶצֶת תָּחַת`); *rḫp* of Anat "hovering"
   (1.108:8; Gen 1:2, Deut 32:11) [cognate]; the seven-day count of the fire in Baal's palace (1.4 VI:24–32 `בַּיּוֹם
   הַשְּׁבִיעִי`; Gen 2:2) [divergence: the seventh day ends a work, not a rest].

**Further key parallels**
4. **Kingship won at the sea:** 1.2 IV:10 "you will take your eternal kingship" after Yam's defeat ↔ Ex 15:18, Ps
   145:13, 29:10, 93:1–4; the step-by-step line of 1.2 IV:9 ↔ Ps 92:10; the clubs "named" by Kothar ↔ the divine
   naming; Habakkuk 3:8 and Nahum 1:4 (`גּוֹעֵר בַּיָּם`) against a goddess who *rebukes* Baal (*gʿr*).
5. **Anat destroys Mot with Moses' verbs** (1.6 II:31–35): *šrp* `שָׂרַף`, *ṭḥn* `טָחַן`, *ḏry* `זָרָה` – the three
   verbs of the golden calf (Ex 32:20) [equivalent].
6. **The Rephaim and Og's towns:** 1.6 VI:46 `הָרְפָאִים`; 1.108:2–3 Rapiʾu "who dwells in Ashtaroth, who rules in
   Edrei" ↔ Deut 1:4, Josh 12:4, Deut 3:11 (Og, the last of the Rephaim).
7. **Small words with large echoes:** 1.24:7 `הֵן, הָעַלְמָה תֵּלֵד בֵּן` ↔ Isa 7:14; *mhr* `מֹהַר` (1.24; Gen 34:12,
   Ex 22:16) and *ṯlḫ* `שִׁלּוּחִים` (1 Kgs 9:16); *mrzḥ* `מַרְזֵחַ` (1.114, 1.1 IV; Amos 6:7, Jer 16:5); *ʿz* // *ḏmr*
   (1.108; Ex 15:2 `עָזִּי וְזִמְרָת`); "one lip to earth, one to heaven" (1.5 II:2, 1.23) and *lšnm tlhk* (1.83:5) ↔
   Ps 73:9; *ʿzm kmt* ↔ Song 8:6 `עַזָּה כַמָּוֶת` (1.6 VI:17); Mot's mourning rites (1.5 VI – 1.6 I) ↔ Gen 37:35, Mic
   1:10, Deut 14:1, Jer 48:37.
8. **A famous parallel that is not on the tablet:** 1.23:14 is *ṭb gd b ḥlb* "good coriander in milk" – no kid and no
   mother (Ratner & Zuckerman 1986): Ex 23:19, 34:26, Deut 14:21 are tagged [absent], with the history in the apparatus.

**Readings corrected against the machine parse** (each a note in its line): *ʿn* "furrow" `מַעֲנָה` (Ps 129:3), not
"Anat" (1.6 IV:1–2, 12–13); *mhr* "bride-price", not "warrior" (1.24); *rḫp*, not *rḥp* (1.108:8); *l* asseverative in
1.3 III:38–46.

**The ancient mind** (the הערות): a god's death and the mourning of El and Anat, gashing and dust; El drunk at his
*marzeaḥ*, the dog under the table and the hangover cure (1.114); a mare who calls god after god, city after city,
until Horon answers (1.100); a marriage negotiated with a bride-price and refused rivals (1.24); a palace that a god must
beg permission to build, with a window that the rains need.
